M_{g}=\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
with polar coordinates
(r,\theta)
and metric

\mathrm{d}s_{g}^{2}=\mathrm{d}r^{2}+g(r)^{2}(\,,\,)_{1},

where
(\,,\,)_{1}
is the standard round metric on the unit sphere. Let

v_{g}(s)=\mathrm{vol}(\mathbb{S}^{m-1})g(s)^{m-1}

be the volume growth of the set
\{r=s\}
. By comparison theory for
\Delta r
(see Appendix A), given a radial bound for the function
b
of the type
b(x)\geq\beta(r(x))
, for some positive
\beta\in C(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})
, to find solutions of (
2.2
) one is first lead to solve

\big[v_{g}\varphi(w^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}=v_{g}\beta f(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)

(2.3)

on an interval of
\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Furthermore, a solution of (
2.3
) gives rise to a solution of (
2.2
) provided the sign of
w^{\prime}
matches appropriately with the inequalities coming from the comparison theorems for
\Delta r
, and with the sign of
f(w)
. Therefore, the monotonicity of
w
becomes relevant. We will devote the next section to the study of the ODE (
2.3
).

The investigation of the compact support principle along these lines needs the use of comparison theorems from below, that requires
r
to be smooth, equivalently,
\mathcal{O}
to be a pole. As outlined in the Introduction, in this section we develop a different radialization procedure that uses a “fake distance”
\varrho
modelled on the operator
\Delta_{\varphi}
, in the particular case of the
p
-Laplacian.

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
, and suppose that
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic on
M^{m}
, that is, that there exists a non-constant positive solution
w\in W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M)
of
\Delta_{p}w\leq 0
. From
[
68
,
147
,
146
,
115
]
, we know this to be equivalent to the fact that the
p
-capacity of some (equivalently, each) compact set
K\subset M
, defined as

\mathrm{cap}_{p}(K)=\inf\left\{\int_{M}|\nabla\psi|^{p}\ \ :\ \ \psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M),\ \psi\geq 1\ \text{ on }\,K\right\},

is positive. Furthermore, by
[
70
,
71
]
the non-parabolicity is also equivalent to the existence for each fixed
o\in M
of a positive Green kernel
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)
, that is, a distributional solution of
\Delta_{p}\mathcal{G}_{p}(\cdot,o)=-\delta_{o}
. In other words,
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)
satisfies

\int_{M}|\nabla_{x}\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)|^{p-2}\langle\nabla_{x}\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o),\nabla\psi(x)\rangle\mathrm{d}x=\psi(o)\qquad\forall\,u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M).

The kernel
\mathcal{G}_{p}
was constructed in
[
70
,
71
]
starting with a smooth increasing exhaustion
\{\Omega_{j}\}
of
M
, that is, a family of smooth open sets such that

\displaystyle o\in\Omega_{j}\Subset\Omega_{j+1}\Subset M\quad\text{for each }\,j\geq 1,\qquad\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{j}=M,

and related Green kernels
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}(\cdot,o)
with Dirichlet boundary conditions on
\partial\Omega_{j}
.
The existence of
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
was shown in
[
70
, Thm. 3.19]
for
p\in(1,m]
, and
3
3

3

In
[
71
]
, p. 656 the author observes that
p\leq m
in
[
70
]
has to be assumed because of Definition 3.9 therein, where
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
is required to diverge as
x\rightarrow o
.
in
[
71
]
for
p>m
.

Remark 2.1
.

The local behavior of a positive solution
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{p}
of
\Delta_{p}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{p}=-\delta_{o}
on a domain
\Omega\subset M
has been investigated in
[
139
, Thm 12]
for
p\leq m
:

\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{p}(x,o)\asymp\int_{r(x)}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{[\omega_{m-1}s^{m-1}]^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\sim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\omega_{m-1}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\left(\frac{p-1}{m-p}\right)r(x)^{-\frac{m-p}{p-1}}&\qquad\text{if }\,p<m,\\[8.5359pt]
\omega_{m-1}^{-\frac{1}{m-1}}|\log r|&\qquad\text{if }\,p=m\end{array}\right.

(2.4)

as
r(x)=\mathrm{dist}(x,o)\rightarrow 0
. For the Euclidean space, in
[
81
,
150
]
the authors proved a stronger estimate with the asymptotic
\sim
in place of
\asymp
in (
2.4
), and their argument is extended to a manifold setting in the forthcoming
[
94
]
. On the other hand, when
p>m
,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{p}
admits a continuous extension to
x=o
with a positive, finite value, since in this case the point
o
has positive capacity (see
[
68
, Thm. 6.33]
and
[
71
]
). By the maximum principle,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{p}(o,o)=\|\mathcal{G}_{p}(\cdot,o)\|_{\infty}
.

The uniqueness of
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
was shown in
[
70
, Thm. 3.22]
for
p=m
, while if
p>m
it is a consequence of the standard comparison for
W^{1,p}
solutions, since in this range of
p
it is known that
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\in W^{1,p}(\Omega_{j})
(see also the discussion at p. 656 of
[
71
]
). If
p\in(1,m]
, one can easily deduce the uniqueness of
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
again by comparison, taking into account the refinements of the asymptotic behaviour near
o
described in Remark
2.1
.

By
[
70
, Thm. 3.25]
, the sequence
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\}
can be arranged to be increasing
4
4

4

As for the uniqueness of
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
, the increasiness of the sequence
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\}
easily follows by comparison in view of the refinements of the asymptotic behaviour in Remark
2.1
.
, and by
[
70
, Thm. 3.27]
it converges to a finite limit if and only if
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic on
M
.

Fix a model manifold
M_{g}
such that

g\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}),\quad g>0\quad\text{on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+},\quad g(0)=0,\quad g^{\prime}(0)=1,

and assume that
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic on
M_{g}
; it is well-known that this is equivalent to require

v_{g}^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\in L^{1}(\infty),

(2.5)

and in fact

\mathcal{G}^{(g)}_{p}(r)=\int_{r}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{v_{g}(s)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}},\qquad r\in\mathbb{R}^{+},

(2.6)

when the integral exists, is the minimal positive Green kernel of
\Delta_{p}
on
M_{g}
with pole at
\{r=0\}
. Fix an origin
o\in M
, and define implicitly
\varrho(x)
on
M\backslash\{o\}
as follows:

-

If
p\in(1,m]
set

\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)=\mathcal{G}^{(g)}_{p}\big(\varrho(x)\big)\qquad\mbox{on }\,M\backslash\{o\},

(2.7)

and note that, because of (
2.4
) and
\mathcal{G}^{(g)}_{p}(0^{+})=+\infty
,
\varrho
is well defined and can be extended continuously on
M
by setting
\varrho(o)=0
.

-

If
p>m
set

L\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)=\mathcal{G}^{(g)}_{p}\big(\varrho(x)\big)\qquad\mbox{on }\,M\backslash\{o\},

(2.8)

with

L=\frac{\mathcal{G}^{(g)}_{p}(0)}{\lim_{x\rightarrow o}\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)}>0.

In this way, by the maximum principle
\varrho(x)
is still well defined on the whole of
M\backslash\{o\}
, positive therein and has a continuous extension to
M
with
\varrho(o)=0
.

Up to replacing
\mathcal{G}_{p}
with
L\mathcal{G}_{p}
when
p>m
, we can consider
\mathcal{G}_{p}
as being defined by (
2.7
) for each
p\in(1,\infty)
. Indeed, in what follows we do not need to use the property
\Delta_{p}\mathcal{G}_{p}=-\delta_{o}
, but only that
\Delta_{p}\mathcal{G}_{p}=0
outside of
o
.

If
M=M_{g}
and
o
is the reference point
\{r=0\}
of
M_{g}
,
\varrho=r
is the distance function on
M
from
o
. In general, the relation between
\varrho
and
r
depends on that between
M
and
M_{g}
, which we now investigate (see also
[
94
]
). By elliptic regularity,
\varrho\in C^{1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M\backslash\{o\})
since so is
\mathcal{G}_{p}
, see
[
145
]
. Differentiating (
2.7
) we obtain

|\nabla\varrho|=v_{g}(\varrho)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{p}|=\left[v_{g}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{\varrho}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{v_{g}(s)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\right]|\nabla\log\mathcal{G}_{p}|.

(2.9)

According to
[
14
]
, we define the critical curve
\chi_{g}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
by setting

\chi_{g}(t)=\left(\frac{p-1}{p}\right)^{p}\left[v_{g}(t)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{v_{g}(s)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\right]^{-p}=\left[\left(-\frac{p-1}{p}\log\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{v_{g}(s)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}}\right)^{\prime}\right]^{p},

(2.10)

and in this way (
2.9
) can be rewritten in the form

|\nabla\varrho|=\frac{p-1}{p}\chi_{g}(\varrho)^{-1/p}|\nabla\log\mathcal{G}_{p}|.

(2.11)

Remark 2.2
.

The critical curve in (
2.10
) is related, via comparison theory, to weights in Hardy-type inequalities on
M
, and it thus appears in geometrical problems where stationary Schrödinger type operators (linear or nonlinear) are considered. A systematic study with various applications has been given in
[
14
,
15
,
16
]
for
p=2
, and in
[
17
]
for general
p
.

A second differentiation gives

\Delta_{p}\varrho=\frac{v_{g}^{\prime}(\varrho)}{v_{g}(\varrho)}|\nabla\varrho|^{p}\qquad\text{weakly on }M\backslash\{o\},

(2.12)

hence, for each
\psi\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R})
with
\psi^{\prime}\neq 0
everywhere we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\Delta_{p}\big[\psi(\varrho)\big]&=&\big|\psi^{\prime}(\varrho)\big|^{p-2}\psi^{\prime}(\varrho)\left[(p-1)\psi^{\prime\prime}(\varrho)+\frac{v_{g}^{\prime}(\varrho)}{v_{g}(\varrho)}\psi^{\prime}(\varrho)\right]|\nabla\varrho|^{p}\\[11.38092pt]
&=&\displaystyle\left[v_{g}^{-1}\left(v_{g}|\psi^{\prime}|^{p-2}\psi^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}\right](\varrho)|\nabla\varrho|^{p}.\end{array}

(2.13)

As we have already observed,
v_{g}^{-1}\left(v_{g}|\psi^{\prime}|^{p-2}\psi^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}
is the expression of the
p
-Laplacian of the radial function
\psi
in the model
M_{g}
, making it possible to radialize with respect to
\varrho
. However, in order for this procedure to be effective we need to control the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
|\nabla\varrho|
and to guarantee properness of
\varrho
. The latter, by (
2.7
), is equivalent to the property
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\rightarrow 0
as
r(x)\rightarrow\infty
.

First we focus our attention on
|\nabla\varrho|
. While bounds from below for
|\nabla\varrho|
seem difficult to obtain, bounds from above are simpler, as shown by the next

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
(M,\langle\,,\,\rangle)
be a complete
m
-dimensional manifold satisfying

\mathrm{Ric}\geq-(m-1)\kappa^{2}\langle\,,\,\rangle

(2.14)

for some
\kappa>0
. Let
p\in(1,\infty)
and assume that
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic. Define
\varrho
as in (
2.7
), with
g(r)=\kappa^{-1}\sinh(\kappa r)
. Then, for each
\varrho_{0}>0
there exists a constant
C_{\varrho}>0
depending on
(m,p,\varrho_{0},\kappa)
and on the geometry of
M
such that

|\nabla\varrho|\leq C_{\varrho}\qquad\text{on }M_{\varrho_{0}}=\big\{x\in M:\varrho(x)\geq\varrho_{0}\big\}.

(2.15)

Proof.

For notational convenience we omit the dependence of
\mathcal{G}_{p}
on
o
. Since
\varrho
is positive on
M\backslash\{o\}
and
\varrho(o)=0
, given
\varrho_{0}>0
there exists
R\in(0,1)
small enough that
M_{\varrho_{0}}
is disjoint from
B_{R}(o)
. The Harnack inequality in
[
153
]
implies, that for each
x\in M_{o}
,

|\nabla\log\mathcal{G}_{p}(x)|\leq C(m,p,R)\left(\frac{1+\kappa r(x)}{r(x)}\right)\leq C_{1}(m,p,R)\frac{1+\kappa r(x)}{1+r(x)},

(2.16)

and using (
2.11
) with
g(r)=g_{\kappa}(r)=\kappa^{-1}\sinh(\kappa r)
we get

|\nabla\varrho(x)|\leq C_{2}(m,p,R)\frac{1+\kappa r(x)}{1+r(x)}\chi_{g_{\kappa}}\big(\varrho(x)\big)^{-1/p}

(2.17)

for each
x\in M_{\varrho_{0}}
. By Proposition 3.12 in
[
14
]
and Example 5.3 in
[
17
]
, if
g/h
is non-decreasing on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
then
\chi_{g}\geq\chi_{h}
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Applying the result with
g=g_{\kappa}
and
h(r)=\exp(\kappa r)
we obtain

\chi_{g_{\kappa}}(t)\geq\chi_{h}(t)\equiv\left(\frac{m-1}{p}\right)^{p}\kappa^{p},

hence inserting into (
2.17
) and maximizing over
r\in[R,\infty)
we deduce (
2.15
).
∎

Remark 2.4
.

Although Proposition
2.3
is enough for our purposes, the upper bound in (
2.15
) could be considerably refined: in view of the fact that
\varrho=r
if
M=M_{g}
, it is reasonable to claim that the sharp bound is
|\nabla\varrho|\leq 1
on the entire
M\backslash\{o\}
. This has been proved by T. Colding in
[
32
]
for
p=2
and
\mathrm{Ric}\geq 0
on
M
, using the Euclidean space as model
M_{g}
, with the equality
|\nabla\varrho|=1
holding at some point if and only if
M=\mathbb{R}^{m}
. In
[
94
]
, the authors extend the sharp bound, with rigidity, to each
p>1
and lower bound (
2.14
), for
\kappa\geq 0
.

The properness of
\varrho
, that is, the property that
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\rightarrow 0
as
r(x)=\mathrm{dist}(x,o)\rightarrow\infty
, is a non-trivial fact intimately related to the geometry of
M
at infinity. Regarding the case when
\mathrm{Ric}\geq 0
, I. Holopainen has proved the following

Theorem 2.5

(Prop. 5.10 of
[
72
]
)
.

Let
M
be complete, and suppose that
\mathrm{Ric}\geq 0
. Denote by
V(r)=\mathrm{vol}(B_{r})
the volume of a geodesic ball of radius
r
centered at some fixed origin
o
, and with
\partial M(r)
the portion of
\partial B_{r}
which is the boundary of an unbounded connected component. Fix
p\in(1,\infty)
. Then,
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic on
M
if and only if

\left(\frac{s}{V(s)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\in L^{1}(\infty).

(2.18)

In this case there exists a constant
C\geq 1
independent of
r
such that

\frac{1}{C}\int_{2r}^{\infty}\left(\frac{s}{V(s)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\leq C\int_{2r}^{\infty}\left(\frac{s}{V(s)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathrm{d}s

(2.19)

for each
x\in\partial M(r)
.

Corollary 2.6
.

Let
p\in(1,\infty)
. If
M
is complete with
\mathrm{Ric}\geq 0
, and
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic, then for each fixed origin
o\in M
the Green kernel
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\rightarrow 0
as
r(x)\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

For the ease of notation we omit the dependence on
o
of
\mathcal{G}_{p}
. Suppose that there exists
c>0
and a sequence
\{x_{j}\}
with
r_{j}=r(x_{j})\rightarrow\infty
such that
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x_{j})\geq c
for each
j
. By (
2.19
), up to removing a finite number of
x_{j}
and relabelling,
x_{j}
necessarily belongs to the boundary of a compact connected component of
M\backslash B_{r_{j}}
. For
r\leq r_{j}
, let
U_{r}
be the connected component of
M\backslash B_{r}
containing
x_{j}
, and define

I_{j}=\Big\{r\in(0,r_{j}]\ :\ \text{$U_{r}$ is compact}\Big\},\qquad\bar{r}_{j}=\inf(I_{j}).

Note that
I_{j}\neq\emptyset
. For
r\in I_{j}
, since
\mathcal{G}_{p}
is
p
-harmonic on
U_{r}
the maximum principle gives
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x_{j})\leq\max_{\partial U_{r}}\mathcal{G}_{p}
, and by continuity
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x_{j})\leq\max_{\partial U_{\bar{r}_{j}}}\mathcal{G}_{p}
. Choose
\hat{r}_{j}\in(\bar{r}_{j}-1,\bar{r}_{j})
in order to satisfy
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x_{j})\leq\max_{\partial U_{\hat{r}_{j}}}\mathcal{G}_{p}+c/2
. Applying (
2.19
) to points of
\partial U_{\hat{r}_{j}}
we have

c\leq\mathcal{G}_{p}(x_{j})\leq\max_{\partial U_{\hat{r}_{j}}}\mathcal{G}_{p}+\frac{c}{2}\leq\frac{c}{2}+C\int_{2\hat{r}_{j}}^{\infty}\left(\frac{s}{V(s)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\mathrm{d}s.

This implies that
\{\hat{r}_{j}\}
, and hence
\{\bar{r}_{j}\}
, is bounded. Fix
R>\sup_{j}\bar{r}_{j}
. Since
r(x_{j})\rightarrow\infty
and using that, by construction, each
x_{j}
belongs to a bounded connected component of
M\backslash B_{R}
, we deduce that
M\backslash B_{R}
should necessarily have infinitely many connected components, a contradiction.
∎

If the Ricci tensor is negative somewhere, due to the possible presence of
p
-parabolic ends some additional condition must be placed on
M
in order to guarantee that
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\rightarrow 0
as
r(x)\rightarrow\infty
. We recall that given
K\subset U\subset M
with
K
compact,
U
open, the
p
-capacity of the condenser
(K,U)
is

\mathrm{cap}_{p}(K,U)=\inf\left\{\int_{M}|\nabla\psi|^{p}\ \ :\ \ \psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(U),\ \psi\geq 1\ \text{ on }\,K\right\}.

Note that
\mathrm{cap}_{p}(K,U)
increases by enlarging
K
or reducing
U
. If
K
is the closure of a smooth open set and
U
is smooth and relatively compact, the infimum is realized by the unique solution of
\Delta_{p}u=0
on
U\backslash K
,
u=1
on
\partial K
and
u=0
on
\partial U
, extended with
u=1
on
K
. We prove the following

Theorem 2.7
.

Suppose that
M
is complete and satisfies a Sobolev inequality

\left(\int|\psi|^{\frac{\nu p}{\nu-p}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-p}{\nu}}\leq S_{p,\nu}\int|\nabla\psi|^{p}\qquad\forall\,\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M),

(2.20)

for some
p\in(1,\infty)
,
\nu>p
and
S_{p,\nu}>0
. Then,
\Delta_{p}
is non-parabolic and there exists a constant
C
depending on
p,\nu
and on the geometry of
M
such that

\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\leq Cr(x)^{-\frac{\nu-p}{p}}\qquad\text{for }\,r(x)\geq 2.

In particular,
\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\rightarrow 0
as
r(x)\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

It is well-known that (
2.20
) implies the non-parabolicity of
\Delta_{p}
on
M
(see for instance
[
115
]
for general
p
, and
[
113
, Lemma 7.13]
for
p=2
). By
[
70
]
,
\mathcal{G}_{p}
is the locally uniform limit of an increasing sequence
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
of Green kernels associated to a smooth increasing exhaustion
\{\Omega_{j}\}
of
M
, with zero boundary conditions. Without loss of generality, assume that
\Omega_{1}\Subset B_{1}(o)\Subset\Omega_{2}
. Hereafter, geodesic balls will always be centered at
o
. Extend each
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
with zero on
M\backslash\Omega_{j}
, and observe that
\Delta_{p}\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq 0
on
M\backslash\{o\}
. By the maximum principle,

\sup_{M\backslash\Omega_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{p,j}=\max_{\partial\Omega_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\leq\max_{\partial\Omega_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{p}=\frac{c_{1}}{2},

and passing to the limit,
\sup_{M\backslash\Omega_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{p}\leq c_{1}/2
. Again by the maximum principle and the monotonicity of the sequence
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\}
,
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\}\subset\{\mathcal{G}_{p}\geq c_{1}\}\subset\Omega_{1}
for each
j
. Moreover,
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}/c_{1}
is the capacitor of the condenser
(\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\},\Omega_{j})
, thus

\int_{\Omega_{j}\backslash\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\}}|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{p,j}|^{p}=c_{1}^{p}\mathrm{cap}_{p}\big(\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\},\Omega_{j}\big)\leq c_{1}^{p}\mathrm{cap}_{p}\big(\overline{\Omega}_{1},\Omega_{2}\big)

for each
j\geq 2
. Plugging in the Sobolev inequality (
2.20
) the text function
\psi_{j}\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M)
given by
\psi_{j}=\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
on
M\backslash\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\}
,
\psi_{j}=c_{1}
on
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\}
, we deduce

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\left(\int_{M\backslash\Omega_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{p,j}^{\frac{\nu p}{\nu-p}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-p}{\nu}}&\leq&\displaystyle\left(\int\psi_{j}^{\frac{\nu p}{\nu-p}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-p}{\nu}}\leq S_{p,\nu}\int|\nabla\psi_{j}|^{p}\\[11.38092pt]
&=&\displaystyle S_{p,\nu}\int_{\Omega_{j}\backslash\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\geq c_{1}\}}|\nabla\mathcal{G}_{p,j}|^{p}\leq S_{p,\nu}c_{1}^{p}\mathrm{cap}_{p}\big(\overline{\Omega}_{1},\Omega_{2}\big).\end{array}

(2.21)

Thus,
\{\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\}
has uniformly bounded
L^{\nu p/(\nu-p)}
-norm on
M\backslash\Omega_{1}
. We now perform a standard Moser’s iteration on annuli to deduce the uniform decay estimate. In what follows, for convenience we write
\mathcal{G}
instead of
\mathcal{G}_{p,j}
. Fix
\gamma\geq 1
, and let
\phi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M\backslash\Omega_{1})
to be chosen later. We compute

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\int\big|\nabla(\phi\mathcal{G}^{\gamma})\big|^{p}&\leq&\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\big|\mathcal{G}^{\gamma}|\nabla\phi|+\gamma\phi\mathcal{G}^{\gamma-1}|\nabla\mathcal{G}|\big|^{p}\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle 2^{p}\int\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}|\nabla\phi|^{p}+2^{p}\gamma^{p}\int\mathcal{G}^{p(\gamma-1)}\phi^{p}|\nabla\mathcal{G}|^{p}.\end{array}

(2.22)

To estimate the second integral on the right-hand, we plug the test function
\phi^{p}\mathcal{G}^{p(\gamma-1)+1}
in the weak definition of
\Delta_{p}\mathcal{G}\geq 0
, and use Schwarz and Young inequalities with parameter
\varepsilon>0
, to obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\int\phi^{p}\mathcal{G}^{p(\gamma-1)}|\nabla\mathcal{G}|^{p}&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{-p}{p(\gamma-1)+1}\int|\nabla\mathcal{G}|^{p-2}\phi^{p-1}\mathcal{G}^{p(\gamma-1)+1}\langle\nabla\mathcal{G},\nabla\phi\rangle\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{p\varepsilon^{p^{\prime}}}{p(\gamma-1)+1}\int\phi^{p}\mathcal{G}^{p(\gamma-1)}|\nabla\mathcal{G}|^{p}+\frac{p\varepsilon^{-p}}{p(\gamma-1)+1}\int\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}|\nabla\phi|^{p}\end{array}

Choosing
\varepsilon
such that

\frac{p\varepsilon^{p^{\prime}}}{p(\gamma-1)+1}=\frac{1}{2}

we get

\displaystyle\int\phi^{p}\mathcal{G}^{p(\gamma-1)}|\nabla\mathcal{G}|^{p}\leq\displaystyle\left(\frac{2p}{p(\gamma-1)+1}\right)^{p}\int\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}|\nabla\phi|^{p}.

Inserting into (
2.22
) we infer

\displaystyle\int\big|\nabla(\phi\mathcal{G}^{\gamma})\big|^{p}\leq 2^{p}\left(1+\left[\frac{2p\gamma}{p(\gamma-1)+1}\right]^{p}\right)\int\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}|\nabla\phi|^{p}\leq C_{p}\int\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}|\nabla\phi|^{p},

for some
C_{p}>0
independent of
\gamma\geq 1
. Using the Sobolev inequality (
2.20
) we then obtain

\displaystyle\left(\int(\phi\mathcal{G}^{\gamma})^{\frac{\nu p}{\nu-p}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-p}{\nu}}\leq S_{p,\nu}C_{p}\int\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}|\nabla\phi|^{p}.

(2.23)

Fix
0<T<R_{0}<R_{1}<\infty
such that
R_{0}-T>1
, and for
i\geq 0
set

\rho_{i}=R_{0}-T\left(2-\sum_{k=0}^{i}2^{-k}\right),\quad\rho_{\infty}=R_{0},\quad r_{i}=R_{1}+T\left(2-\sum_{k=0}^{i}2^{-k}\right),\quad r_{\infty}=R_{1}.

Define
A_{i}=B_{r_{i}}\backslash B_{\rho_{i}}
, and note that
A_{i}\subset A_{0}\subset M\backslash B_{1}\subset M\backslash\Omega_{1}
. Let
\eta_{i}\in\mathrm{Lip}(\mathbb{R})
which is
1
on
[\rho_{i+1},r_{i+1}]
,
0
outside
[\rho_{i},r_{i}]
and linear in between, and set
\phi_{i}=\eta_{i}(r)
. Inserting
\phi_{i}
in (
2.23
) and using
|\nabla\phi_{i}|\leq T^{-1}2^{i+1}
we deduce

\displaystyle\left(\int_{A_{i+1}}\mathcal{G}^{\gamma\frac{\nu p}{\nu-p}}\right)^{\frac{\nu-p}{\nu}}\leq S_{p,\nu}C_{p}(2^{i+1}/T)^{p}\int_{A_{i}}\mathcal{G}^{\gamma p}.

We use the inequality with

\gamma=\gamma_{i}=\left(\frac{\nu}{\nu-p}\right)^{i+1}>1

to obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}^{p}\|_{L^{\gamma_{i+1}}(A_{i+1})}\leq(S_{p,\nu}C_{p})^{\gamma_{i}^{-1}}(2^{i+1}/T)^{p\gamma_{i}^{-1}}\|\mathcal{G}^{p}\|_{L^{\gamma_{i}}(A_{i})}.

Iterating and computing explicitly the sums,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(A_{\infty})}&\leq&\displaystyle(S_{p,\nu}C_{p}T^{-p})^{\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{i}^{-1}}2^{p\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(i+1)\gamma_{i}^{-1}}\|\mathcal{G}^{p}\|_{L^{\gamma_{0}}(A_{0})}\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle(S_{p,\nu}C_{p})^{\frac{\nu-p}{p}}T^{p-\nu}2^{\frac{\nu(\nu-p)}{p}}\|\mathcal{G}^{p}\|_{L^{\gamma_{0}}(A_{0})}.\end{array}

From (
2.21
),
A_{0}\subset\Omega\backslash\Omega_{1}
and our definition of
\gamma_{0}
we deduce

\|\mathcal{G}^{p}\|_{L^{\gamma_{0}}(A_{0})}\leq S_{p,\nu}c_{1}^{p}\mathrm{cap}_{p}(\overline{\Omega}_{1},\Omega_{2}),

hence

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(A_{\infty})}\leq\displaystyle S_{p,\nu}^{\frac{\nu}{p}}C_{p,\nu}T^{p-\nu}c_{1}^{p}\mathrm{cap}_{p}(\overline{\Omega}_{1},\Omega_{2}).\\

Fix
R>2
and choose
T=R/2
,
R_{0}=R
. Letting
R_{1}\rightarrow\infty
and using again the maximum principle we conclude

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\|_{L^{\infty}(M\backslash B_{R})}\leq\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}_{p,j}\|_{L^{\infty}(A_{\infty})}\leq\displaystyle S_{p,\nu}^{\nu/p^{2}}\hat{C}_{p,\nu}R^{-\frac{\nu-p}{p}}c_{1}\mathrm{cap}_{p}(\overline{\Omega}_{1},\Omega_{2})^{1/p}.

and letting then
j\rightarrow\infty
we obtain the desired decay for
\mathcal{G}_{p}
.
∎

We conclude this section by describing three relevant examples where (
2.20
) holds with
\nu=m
, and consequently for
p\in(1,m)

\mathcal{G}_{p}(x,o)\leq Cr(x)^{-\frac{m-p}{p}}\qquad\text{when }\,r(x)\geq 2.

(2.24)

We stress that, by
[
26
, Prop. 2.5]
and
[
115
]
, (
2.20
) holds on
M
possibly with a different constant
S_{p,\nu}
if and only if it holds outside some compact set of
M
.

Example 2.8
.

We recall that a Cartan-Hadamard space is a complete, simply-connected manifold with non-positive sectional curvature. Let
M^{m}\rightarrow N^{n}
be a complete, minimal immersion into a Cartan-Hadamard space. By
[
69
]
, the
L^{1}
-Sobolev inequality

\left(\int|\psi|^{\frac{m}{m-1}}\right)^{\frac{m-1}{m}}\leq S_{1}\int|\nabla\psi|\qquad\forall\,\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M)

(2.25)

holds for some
S_{1}(m)>0
. Let
p\in(1,m)
. Plugging as test function
|\psi|^{\alpha}
, for
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
and
\alpha=\frac{p(m-1)}{m-p}>1
, using Hölder inequality and rearranging we get (
2.20
) with
\nu=m
. Consequently, by Theorem
2.7
, (
2.24
) holds with the constant
C
depending on
(p,m)
and the geometry of
M
.

Example 2.9
.

Let
M^{m}
be a complete manifold satisfying

(i)

\mathrm{Ric}\geq-(m-1)\kappa^{2}\langle\,,\,\rangle
for some
\kappa>0
, and

\inf_{x\in M}\mathrm{vol}\big(B_{1}(x)\big)=\upsilon>0;

(2.26)

(ii)

for some
p\in(1,m)
and
C_{P}>0
, we have the validity of the Poincaré inequality

\int|\psi|^{p}\leq C_{P}\int|\nabla\psi|^{p}\qquad\forall\,\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M).

(2.27)

By work of N. Varopoulos (see
[
66
]
, Thm. 3.2), because of
(i)

M
enjoys the
L^{1}
-Sobolev inequality

\left(\int|\psi|^{\frac{m}{m-1}}\right)^{\frac{m-1}{m}}\leq S_{1}\int\big[|\nabla\psi|+|\psi|\big]\qquad\forall\,\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M),

(2.28)

for some
S_{1}(m,\kappa,\upsilon)>0
. Using again as a test function
|\psi|^{\alpha}
, for
u\in C^{\infty}_{c}(M)
and
\alpha=\frac{p(m-1)}{m-p}
, by Hölder inequality and rearranging we get (see
[
65
, Lem. 2.1]
)

\left(\int|\psi|^{\frac{mp}{m-p}}\right)^{\frac{m-p}{m}}\leq S_{p}\int\big[|\nabla\psi|^{p}+|\psi|^{p}\big]\qquad\forall\,\psi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(M),

(2.29)

for some
S_{p}(m,\kappa,\upsilon,p)>0
. Assumption
(ii)
then guarantees (
2.20
) with
\nu=m
, and (
2.24
) follows from Theorem
2.7
, with
C
depending on
(m,\kappa,\upsilon,p)
and the geometry of
M
.

Example 2.10
.

Two manifolds
M,N
of the same dimension, with metrics
\mathrm{d}_{M},\mathrm{d}_{N}
, are said to be
roughly isometric
if there exists
\varphi:M\rightarrow N
such that

-

B_{\varepsilon}\big(\varphi(M)\big)=N
for some
\varepsilon>0
;

-

there exist constant
C_{1}\geq 1,C_{2}\geq 0
such that

C_{1}^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{M}(x,y)-C_{2}\leq\mathrm{d}_{N}\big(\varphi(x),\varphi(y)\big)\leq C_{1}\mathrm{d}_{M}(x,y)+C_{2}

for each
x,y\in M
.

Note that in fact
M,N
need not have the same dimension, but in what follows we are not interested in this more general case. M. Kanai in
[
76
]
proved that if
M
and
N
are roughly isometric manifolds both satisfying the uniform condition

(iii)

\mathrm{Ric}\geq-(m-1)\kappa^{2}\langle\,,\,\rangle
, for some
\kappa>0
, and
\mathrm{inj}(M)>0
,

with
\mathrm{inj}(M)
the injectivity radius of
M
, then (
2.25
) holds on
M
if and only if it holds on
N
. In particular, a manifold
M
satisfying
(iii)
and roughly isometric to
\mathbb{R}^{m}
enjoys (
2.25
), and therefore (
2.20
) with
\nu=m
. In the same assumptions note also that
\Delta_{p}
is parabolic for each
p\geq m
, see
[
71
, Thm. 3.16]
, and thus (
2.20
) is false for any
m\leq p<\nu
. We remark in passing that
\mathrm{inj}(M)>0
implies the lower bound of the volume in
(ii)
of Example
2.9
, see
[
34
, Prop. 14]
.

Remark 2.11
.

For
p=2
, Theorem
2.7
and a weaker version of the result in Example
2.9
have been obtained in
[
101
]
by integrating the corresponding decay estimate for the heat kernel.

3
Boundary value problems for nonlinear ODEs

At the beginning of Section
2
, we observed that to find radial solutions of
(P_{\geq})
and
(P_{\leq})
one is lead to solve the following ODE:

\big[v_{g}\varphi(w^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}=v_{g}\beta f(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)

(3.1)

on an interval of
\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
, where we have extended
\varphi
to an odd function on
\mathbb{R}
. The functions
v_{g}
and
\beta
are bounds, respectively, for the volume of geodesic spheres of
M
and for
b
. We devote this section to the study of (
3.1
). Regarding
\varphi,f,l
we assume the following:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\mbox{$\varphi\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})$, $\ \varphi(0)=0$, $\ \varphi(t)>0$ for all $t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}$,}\\[5.69046pt]
\displaystyle\text{$\varphi$ is strictly increasing on $\mathbb{R}^{+}$,}\\[5.69046pt]
\displaystyle f\in C(\mathbb{R}),\\[5.69046pt]
f\geq 0\quad\text{ in $(0,\eta_{0})$, for some $\eta_{0}\in(0,\infty)$,}\\[5.69046pt]
\displaystyle l\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}),\quad l\geq 0\ \text{ in }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}.\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}\right.

(3.2)

We point out that no monotonicity is needed neither on
f
nor on
l
. In some results, we also require the validity of the next conditions:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\mbox{$\varphi\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+})$, $\ \varphi^{\prime}>0$ on $\mathbb{R}^{+}$,}\\[8.5359pt]
\displaystyle\frac{t\varphi^{\prime}(t)}{l(t)}\in L^{1}(0^{+}).\end{array}\right.

(3.3)

Set

\varphi(\infty)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\varphi(t)\in(0,\infty],

and define
K
and
F
respectively as in (
1.19
) and (
1.20
).

In what follows, we find convenient to normalize the interval where we study (
3.1
), say on
[0,T_{0}]
, and for this reason we introduce two functions
a,\wp
that will be related to, respectively,
\beta
and
v_{g}
, in a way that may depend from the geometrical problem at hand. We require

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\wp\in C^{1}([0,T_{0}]),&\qquad\wp>0\ \text{ on }[0,T_{0}],\\[2.84544pt]
a\in C([0,T_{0}]),&\qquad a>0\ \text{ on }\,[0,T_{0}].\end{array}\right.

(3.4)

3.1
The Dirichlet problem

We first investigate the existence and the qualitative properties of
C^{1}
weak solutions of the singular boundary value problem

\begin{cases}[\wp\,\varphi(w^{\prime})]^{\prime}=\wp af(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)\quad\mbox{on }\,(0,T),\\[2.84544pt]
w(0)=0,\quad w(T)=\eta,\\[2.84544pt]
0\leq w\leq\eta,\quad w^{\prime}\geq 0\ \text{on }\,(0,T)\end{cases}

(3.5)

where
\eta>0
,
T\in(0,T_{0})
are given.
The results of this section are inspired by Chapters 4 and 8 of
[
123
]
, and we also borrow some of the main ideas of the proof of Proposition 3.1 in
[
57
]
and the appendix of Chapter 4 in
[
123
]
, but with several improvements in the spirit of
[
21
, Thm. 4.1]
.

One of the main points in our investigation is to determine under which assumptions on
f
,
l
,
\varphi
and
a
, solutions of (
3.5
) satisfy
w^{\prime}(0)=0
or
w^{\prime}(0)>0
, that is, whether or not
w
can be pasted to the zero function on
(-\infty,0)
in a
C^{1}
way. As we shall see, such assumptions will be substantially given by the integrability condition (
KO0
).

For
\eta,\xi>0
set

\begin{array}[]{ll}a_{0}=\min_{[0,T_{0}]}a,&\qquad a_{1}=\max_{[0,T_{0}]}a;\\[5.69046pt]
\wp_{0}=\min_{[0,T_{0}]}\wp,&\qquad\wp_{1}=\max_{[0,T_{0}]}\wp;\\[5.69046pt]
f_{\eta}=\max_{[0,\eta]}f,&\qquad l_{\xi}=\max_{[0,\xi]}l;\\[5.69046pt]
\displaystyle\Theta(T)=\sup_{[0,T]}\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\int_{0}^{t}\wp(s)a(s)\mathrm{d}s.\end{array}

(3.6)

Note that
\Theta(T)\rightarrow 0
as
T\rightarrow 0
.

We aim to prove the following existence

Theorem 3.1
.

Assume (
3.2
), (
3.4
) and

f(0)l(0)=0.

(3.7)

Fix
\xi>0
, let
T\in(0,T_{0})
and
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, with
\eta_{0}
as in (
3.2
), satisfying

\frac{\wp_{1}}{\wp_{0}}\varphi\left(\frac{\eta}{T}\right)+2\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}<\varphi(\xi).

(3.8)

Then, problem (
3.5
) admits a weak solution
w\in C^{1}([0,T])
such that

0\leq w^{\prime}\leq\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\wp_{1}}{\wp_{0}}\varphi\left(\frac{\eta}{T}\right)+2\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\right)

(3.9)

In particular,
0\leq w^{\prime}<\xi
.

We begin with the following auxiliary result, see Lemma 4.1.3 of
[
123
]
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Under assumptions (
3.2
) and (
3.4
), suppose that
\varphi
is extended on all of
\mathbb{R}
in such a way that
t\varphi(t)>0
on
\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
. Then, any weak solution
w\in C^{1}([0,T])
of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathrm{sign}(w)\cdot\big[\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}\geq 0\qquad\text{in }\,(0,T),\\[5.69046pt]
w(0)=0,\quad w(T)=\eta>0\end{array}\right.

(3.10)

is such that

w\geq 0,\qquad w^{\prime}\geq 0\qquad\text{in }[0,T].

(3.11)

Moreover, there exists
t_{0}\in[0,T)
such that

w\equiv 0\ \ \text{ in }\,[0,t_{0}];\qquad w>0,\ w^{\prime}>0\ \ \text{ in }(t_{0},T],

(3.12)

Proof.

We first claim that
w\geq 0
on
[0,T]
. Otherwise, by contradiction there exist
t_{0}
,
t_{1}
, with
0\leq t_{0}<t_{1}<T
, such that
w(t_{0})=w(t_{1})=0
and
w<0
on
(t_{0},t_{1})
. Using the non-negative, Lipschitz test function
\psi=-w
on
[t_{0},t_{1}]
,
\psi=0
otherwise, we get

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})w^{\prime}\leq 0.

Since
s\varphi(s)>0
in
\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
by assumption, the integrand is strictly positive. This gives the desired contradiction.

Let
\mathcal{J}=\{t\in(0,T):w^{\prime}(t)>0\}
. Since
w\in C^{1}([0,T])
and
w(T)>w(0)
,
\mathcal{J}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{J}
is open in
(0,T)
. Let
t_{0}=\inf\mathcal{J}\in[0,T)
, so that
w\equiv 0
on
[0,t_{0}]
. For each fixed
t\in(t_{0},T)
, there necessarily exists
\bar{t}\in(t_{0},t)
with
w^{\prime}(\bar{t})>0
. Integrating (
3.10
) on
[\bar{t},t]
we deduce that
\wp(t)\varphi(w^{\prime}(t))\geq\wp(\bar{t})\varphi(w^{\prime}(\bar{t}))>0
, which imply that
w^{\prime}(t)>0
. Hence,
w^{\prime}>0
on
(t_{0},T]
. Integrating again we obtain
w>0
on
(t_{0},T]
, concluding the proof.
∎

Remark 3.3
.

Note that the a-priori knowledge of
w\geq 0
in
[0,T]
allows us to directly apply the second part of the proof of Lemma
3.2
and conclude that
w^{\prime}\geq 0
on
(0,T)
, and so
0\leq w\leq\eta
.

We are now ready to solve the singular two-points boundary value problem (
3.5
).

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Redefine
f
and
l
on the complementary of, respectively,
[0,\eta]
and
[0,\xi]
, in such a way that

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle 0\leq f(s)\leq f_{\eta}\quad\text{ for }\,s\geq\eta,\qquad f(s)=0\quad\text{ for }\,s<0,\\[5.69046pt]
0<l(s)\leq l_{\xi}\quad\text{ for }\,s\geq\xi\end{array}

(3.13)

Note that we can change
l
as above still keeping the validity of
l\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})
, while, with this procedure, we can only ensure that
f\in C(\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\})
, since
f
has a jump discontinuity at
s=0
when
f(0)>0
. The modifications will not affect the conclusions of the theorem since any ultimate solution with
w^{\prime}\geq 0
satisfies
0\leq w\leq\eta
and
|w^{\prime}|\leq\xi
. However, the region
\{w=0\}
needs a special care. We extend
\varphi
to a continuous function defined on all of
\mathbb{R}
in such a way that

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\varphi<0\quad\text{on }\,(-\infty,0),\qquad\varphi(t)=-\varphi(-t)\qquad\text{if }\,t\in\left[-\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\eta}{T}\right),0\right],\\[5.69046pt]
\varphi\qquad\text{is strictly increasing on }\,\mathbb{R};\\[5.69046pt]
\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\varphi(t)=-\infty.\end{array}

(3.14)

Let

\mu_{1}=\frac{\wp_{1}}{\wp_{0}}\varphi\left(\frac{\eta}{T}\right)+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}<\varphi(\xi)

(3.15)

by (
3.8
), and set

I=[-\wp_{1}\mu_{1},\wp_{0}\mu_{1}].

To show the existence of solutions of (
3.5
), following the approach in
[
123
]
we use Browder’s version of the Leray-Schauder theorem
5
5

5

The idea is attributed by the authors in
[
123
]
to M. Montenegro.
(see Theorem 11.6 of
[
58
]
), for the parametric family of boundary value problems

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\big[\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}=\sigma\wp af(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)\qquad\text{on }(0,T),\\[5.69046pt]
w(0)=0,\qquad w(T)=\sigma\eta\geq 0,\end{array}\right.

(3.16)

for
\sigma\in[0,1]
. In our case, however, the presence of a nonconstant
l
makes things more subtle. To tackle the problem we let
X
be the Banach space
X=(C^{1}([0,T]),\|\cdot\|)
, where

\|w\|=\|w\|_{\infty}+\|w^{\prime}\|_{\infty}
for
w\in X
. Define
\mathcal{H}:X\times[0,1]\rightarrow X
as follows:

\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)(t)=\sigma\eta-\int_{t}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\wp(s)}\left[\delta+\sigma\int_{0}^{s}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right]\right)\mathrm{d}s,

(3.17)

where
\delta=\delta(w,\sigma)\in I
and
\delta
is chosen in such a way that

\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)(0)=0.

(3.18)

We claim that such a choice of
\delta
is possible, and in fact it is unique. First, we check that
\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)
is well-defined for each fixed
(w,\sigma)\in X\times[0,1]
and
\delta\in I
. This follows from the next chain of inequalities, where we use the definition of
\Theta(T)
and (
3.8
):

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\wp(s)}\left[\delta+\sigma\int_{0}^{s}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right]\leq\frac{\wp_{0}\mu_{1}}{\wp_{0}}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\\[11.38092pt]
\qquad\displaystyle=\mu_{1}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}<\varphi(\xi).\end{array}

(3.19)

We remark that, by construction,
\varphi
is a homeomorphism of
(-\infty,0)
onto itself. Moreover, if
\delta=-\wp_{1}\mu_{1}
, recalling that
\varphi^{-1}
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)(0)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\sigma\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\wp(s)}\left[-\wp_{1}\mu_{1}+\sigma\int_{0}^{s}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right]\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq\displaystyle\sigma\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(-\frac{\wp_{1}\mu_{1}}{\wp(s)}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq\sigma\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(-\mu_{1}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\right)\mathrm{d}s\geq\sigma\eta,

where the last inequality follows from (
3.14
) and since
\mu_{1}\geq\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}
. On the other hand, for
\delta=\wp_{0}\mu_{1}
, for all
(w,\sigma)\in X\times[0,1]
we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)(0)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\sigma\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\wp(s)}\left[\wp_{0}\mu_{1}+\sigma\int_{0}^{s}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right]\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\wp_{0}\mu_{1}}{\wp(s)}\right)\leq\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\wp_{1}}{\wp(s)}\varphi\left(\frac{\eta}{T}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\eta-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{-1}\left(\varphi\left(\frac{\eta}{T}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s=0.

Now, the integrand in the RHS of (
3.17
) is a strictly increasing function of
\delta
for
(w,\sigma)
fixed. It is therefore clear that there exists a unique
\delta=\delta(w,\sigma)\in I
such that
\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)(0)=0
.

It remains to show that a fixed point of
w=\mathcal{H}(w,1)
exists. To apply Browder’s version of the Leray–Schauder theorem, we shall check that:

(i)

\mathcal{H}(w,0)=0
, the zero function of
X
,

(ii)

\mathcal{H}:X\times[0,1]\rightarrow X
is continuous and compact,

(iii)

There exists a constant
\Lambda>0
such that
\|w\|\leq\Lambda

for all
(w,\sigma)\in X\times[0,1]
, with
w=\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)
.

Property
(i)
is immediate by the definition of
\mathcal{H}
, since
\delta(w,0)=0
for all
w\in X
. Regarding
(iii)
, by construction each solution of
w=\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)
is of class
C^{1}([0,T])
and has the property that
\varphi(w^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Lip}([0,T])
.
We claim that
w\geq 0
on
[0,T]
. Indeed, if
w<0
somewhere, fix an interval
(t_{1},t_{2})\subset(0,T)
such that
w<0
on
(t_{1},t_{2})
,
w(t_{1})=w(t_{2})=0
. From
f=0
on
(-\infty,0)
we deduce
\big(\varphi(w^{\prime})\big)^{\prime}=0
on
(t_{1},t_{2})
; thus, integrating against the test function
(w+\varepsilon)_{-}
and letting
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
we get

0=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})w^{\prime},

and because of the positivity of
s\varphi(s)
on
\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\}
we deduce that
w^{\prime}
, and consequently
w
, vanishes identically on
(t_{1},t_{2})
, contradiction. By Remark
3.3
, from
w\geq 0
in
[0,T]
we infer
w^{\prime}\geq 0
and
0\leq w\leq\eta
in
[0,T]
, and
the identity

w^{\prime}(t)=[\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)]^{\prime}(t)=\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\left[\delta+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right]\right)

(3.20)

implies

\begin{array}[]{lcl}0\leq w^{\prime}(t)&\leq&\displaystyle\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\delta}{\wp(t)}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\right)\leq\varphi^{-1}\Big(\mu_{1}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\Big)<\xi.\end{array}

(3.21)

Hence, each solution of
w=\mathcal{H}(w,\sigma)
enjoys the a-priori estimate
\|w\|\leq\eta+\xi
, as required.

We are left to prove
(ii)
. Let
\{(w_{k},\sigma_{k})\}_{k}
be a bounded sequence in
X\times[0,1]
, say
\|w_{k}\|\leq L
for all
k
. Using that
\delta_{k}=\delta(w_{k},\sigma_{k})\in I
and
0\leq f(t)\leq f_{\eta}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, together with (
3.19
), we deduce that

\left\|\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\max\left\{\left|\varphi^{-1}\left(-\frac{\wp_{1}\mu_{1}}{\wp_{0}}\right)\right|,\varphi^{-1}\Big(\mu_{1}+\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\Big)\right\},

(3.22)

thus
\big\{\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})\big\}_{k}
is equi-bounded in
X
and equi-continuous in
[0,T]\times[0,1]
. To show the equicontinuity of
\big\{\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})\big\}_{k}
in
C^{1}
, we shall estimate the difference

\big|\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}(t)-\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}(s)\big|

for
0\leq s<t\leq T
. Set for convenience

\displaystyle x_{k}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\left(\delta_{k}+\sigma_{k}\int_{0}^{t}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w_{k}(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}_{k}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle y_{k}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\wp(s)}\left(\delta_{k}+\sigma_{k}\int_{0}^{s}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w_{k}(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}_{k}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right),

and note that

\big|\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}(t)-\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}(s)\big|=\big|\varphi^{-1}(x_{k})-\varphi^{-1}(y_{k})\big|.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and let
\varrho=\varrho(\varphi^{-1},\varepsilon)>0
be the corresponding number of the uniform continuity of
\varphi^{-1}
in
[-\frac{\wp_{1}}{\wp_{0}}\mu_{1},\mu_{1}]
. Set
c=\wp_{1}a_{1}f_{\eta}l_{\xi}
, and suppose that
|t-s|<\varrho/C
, where

C=\frac{\wp_{1}\mu_{1}}{\wp_{0}^{2}}\max_{\tau\in[0,T]}|\wp^{\prime}(\tau)|+\kappa,\quad\kappa=\frac{c}{\wp_{0}}\left(\frac{T}{\wp_{0}}\,\max_{\tau\in[0,T]}|\wp^{\prime}(\tau)|+1\right).

(3.23)

This is possible by (
3.4
), since
\wp\geq\wp_{0}>0
on
[0,T]
and
\wp\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})
. Define

I_{k}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w_{k}(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}_{k}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau,\qquad\mathcal{I}_{k}(t)=\frac{I_{k}(t)}{\wp(t)},

and note that for each
k

0\leq I_{k}(t)-I_{k}(s)\leq c(t-s)\qquad\mbox{and}\quad\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\mathcal{I}_{k}(t)=0.

Using
\wp>0
in
\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
and
\wp\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})
we get

\displaystyle\big|\sigma_{k}\mathcal{I}_{k}(s)-\sigma_{k}\mathcal{I}_{k}(t)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathcal{I}_{k}(s)-\mathcal{I}_{k}(t)\big|=\left|\frac{\wp(t)I_{k}(s)-\wp(s)I_{k}(t)}{\wp(s)\wp(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\wp(t)-\wp(s)|}{\wp(s)\wp(t)}\,I_{k}(s)+\frac{|I_{k}(s)-I_{k}(t)|}{\wp(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\wp_{0}}\left(\frac{T}{\wp_{0}}\,\max_{\tau\in[0,T]}|\wp^{\prime}(\tau)|+1\right)|t-s|=\kappa|t-s|,

Since
\delta_{k}\in I
, by (
3.23
) we estimate

\begin{array}[]{lcl}|x_{k}-y_{k}|&\leq&\displaystyle|\delta_{k}|\frac{|\wp(t)-\wp(s)|}{\wp(s)\wp(t)}+\big|\sigma_{k}\mathcal{I}_{k}(s)-\sigma_{k}\mathcal{I}_{k}(t)\big|\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\left(\frac{\wp_{1}\mu_{1}}{\wp_{0}^{2}}\max_{\tau\in[0,T]}|\wp^{\prime}(\tau)|+\kappa\right)|t-s|=C|t-s|<\varrho.\end{array}

In conclusion,

\big|\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}(t)-\mathcal{H}(w_{k},\sigma_{k})^{\prime}(s)\big|=\big|\varphi^{-1}(x_{k})-\varphi^{-1}(y_{k})\big|<\varepsilon

provided that
|t-s|<\varrho/C
, independently of
k
. As an immediate consequence of the Ascoli–Arzelà theorem
\mathcal{H}
maps bounded sequences of
X\times[0,1]
into relatively compact
sequences of
X
. Finally, if
(w_{k},\sigma_{k})
converges to some
(w,\sigma)
in
X\times[0,1]
, then

\delta_{k}\rightarrow\delta=\delta(w,\sigma)
as
k\to\infty
. Hence,
\mathcal{H}
is continuous in
X\times[0,1]
. The proof of this fact is similar to that given in Chapter 4 of
[
123
]
. The Leray-Schauder fixed point theorem can therefore be applied and the mapping
\mathcal{H}(w,1)
has a fixed point
w
, which by (
3.21
) satisfies inequality (
3.9
).

To conclude, we prove that
w
solves the ODE in (
3.5
) with the original
f,l
. Differentiating (
3.20
) with
\sigma=1
, we see that
w
is a weak solution of

\big(\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})\big)^{\prime}=\wp a\bar{f}(w)\bar{l}(|w^{\prime}|)\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T),

(3.24)

where now we have denoted with
\bar{f},\bar{l}
the modifications of
f,l
in (
3.13
). Clearly,
\bar{l}(w^{\prime})=l(w^{\prime})
on
(0,T)
, since
0\leq w^{\prime}<\xi
. From
0\leq w\leq\eta
and Lemma
3.2
, there exists
t_{0}\in[0,T)
such that
w=0
on
[0,t_{0}]
and
w>0
,
w^{\prime}>0
on
(t_{0},T]
, and since
\bar{f}=f
on
(0,\eta)
we deduce

\big(\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})\big)^{\prime}=\wp af(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)

(3.25)

on
(t_{0},T)
. On the other hand, because of our assumption
f(0)l(0)=0
the function
w=0
solves (
3.25
) on
(0,t_{0})
. Since
w\in C^{1}([0,T])
, (
3.25
) holds weakly on all of
(0,T)
, as claimed.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

The requirement
f(0)l(0)=0
is crucial for the validity of the above theorem, because otherwise
w
might be negative somewhere.

Given
f,\varphi,l
satisfying (
3.2
), define
K,F
as in (
1.19
), (
1.20
). We next present two auxiliary calculus results. The first is similar to Lemma 4.4.1–
(i)
in
[
123
]
. We recall that the notion of a
C
-increasing function is given in Definition
1.9
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Assume that
f
is
C
-increasing in
(0,\eta_{0})
, for some
\eta_{0}>0
. Then, for each
\sigma\in[0,1]
we have
F(\sigma t)\leq C\,\sigma F(t)
for all
t\in[0,\eta_{0})
.

Proof.

Fix
\sigma\in[0,1]
. Since
f
is
C
-increasing in
(0,\eta_{0})
, we have
\sigma f(\sigma t)\leq C\,\sigma f(t)
for each
t\in[0,\eta_{0})
, and thus

F(\sigma t)=\int_{0}^{\sigma t}f(s)\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma f(\sigma\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\leq C\,\sigma\int_{0}^{t}f(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau=C\sigma F(t),

as claimed.
∎

The second lemma concerns the preservation of the validity of (
KO0
) and (

KO
∞

) when we replace
f
with
\sigma f
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Similar results have been proved in
[
123
, Lem. 4.1.2]
,
[
57
]
(remark on page 523) and
[
95
]
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
f,l
satisfy (
3.2
), (
3.3
) and suppose
l>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

(i)

Assume that
f
is positive and
C
-increasing in
(0,\eta_{0})
, for
\eta_{0}>0
in (
3.2
). Then

\frac{1}{K^{-1}(F(s))}\in L^{1}(0^{+})\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\frac{1}{K^{-1}(\sigma F(s))}\in L^{1}(0^{+})

for some (equivalently, any)
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

(ii)

Assume that
f
is positive and
C
-increasing on
(\bar{\eta}_{0},\infty)
, for some
\bar{\eta}_{0}>0
. Having defined
F(t)=\int_{\bar{\eta}_{0}}^{t}f
, it holds

\frac{1}{K^{-1}(F(s))}\in L^{1}(\infty)\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\frac{1}{K^{-1}(\sigma F(s))}\in L^{1}(\infty)

for some (equivalently, any)
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Proof.

For the ease of notation we denote with
\text{(KO${}_{0}$)}(\sigma)
and
\text{(KO${}_{\infty}$)}(\sigma)
, respectively, the integrability conditions

\int_{0^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(\sigma F(s))}<\infty,\qquad\int^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(\sigma F(s))}<\infty.

We prove
(i)
, beginning with implication

\eqref{KO_zero}\Rightarrow\text{(KO${}_{0}$)}(\sigma).

(3.26)

If
\sigma\geq 1
, (
3.26
) is immediate from the monotonicity of
F
and
K
.

If
\sigma\in(0,1)
, we apply Lemma
3.5
with
\sigma/C
replacing
\sigma
(note that
C\geq 1
) to deduce
F(\sigma t/C)\leq\sigma F(t)
for each
t\in(0,\eta_{0})
. Integrating and changing variables,

\int_{0^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(\sigma F(s))}\leq\int_{0^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(F(\sigma s/C))}=\frac{C}{\sigma}\int_{0^{+}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{K^{-1}(F(\tau))},

which proves (
3.26
). To show the reverse implication in (
3.26
), it is enough to observe that
\text{(KO${}_{0}$)}(\sigma)
is condition (
KO0
) for the function
\bar{f}=\sigma f
, and to apply the previous estimates with
\bar{f}
replacing
f
and
\sigma^{-1}
replacing
\sigma
.

The proof of
(ii)
is analogous.
∎

We are now ready to obtain further information on the solution of problem (
3.5
) given in Theorem
3.1
. First, we investigate sufficient conditions to ensure
w^{\prime}(0)>0
.

Proposition 3.7
.

Assume (
3.2
), (
3.3
) and (
3.4
). Suppose that

f(0)l(0)=0
;

f
is
C
-increasing on
(0,\eta_{0})
, for
\eta_{0}>0
as in (
3.2
),

and that one of the following two sets of conditions is met:

(i)

f\equiv 0
on
(0,\eta_{0})
;

(ii)

f>0
on
(0,\eta_{0})
, and also

-

l
is
C
-increasing on
(0,\xi_{0})
, for some
\xi_{0}>0
.

-

\wp
is monotone on
[0,T_{0}]
, either increasing or decreasing (
T_{0}
as in (
3.4
)),

and

\frac{1}{K^{-1}\circ F}\not\in L^{1}(0^{+}).

(
\neg
KO
0
)

Then, the solution
w
of problem (
3.5
), with
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
,
constructed in Theorem
3.1
, has the further properties

w>0\quad\text{on }(0,T],\qquad w^{\prime}>0\quad\text{on }[0,T].

(3.27)

Proof.

First, observe that if we prove that
w^{\prime}(0)>0
, then (
3.27
) follows by a direct application of Lemma
3.2
. We prove
w^{\prime}(0)>0
for cases
(i)
and
(ii)
separately.

Case
(i)
.

Since
f\equiv 0
on
(0,\eta_{0})
, then
(\wp\varphi(w^{\prime}))^{\prime}=0
in
(0,T)
by (
3.5
) and the choice
\eta<\eta_{0}
. Hence, integrating

\wp(t)\varphi(w^{\prime}(t))=\wp(0)\varphi(w^{\prime}(0))\quad\text{for all }t\in(0,T].

(3.28)

Suppose by contradiction that
w^{\prime}(0)=0
. From (
3.2
) we have
\varphi>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, and also
\wp>0
on
[0,T]
. Thus, (
3.28
) would imply that
w^{\prime}
, hence
w
, is identically zero in
[0,T]
, contradicting
w(T)=\eta
.

Case
(ii)
.

Theorem
3.1
guarantees that
\varphi(w^{\prime})\in C^{1}([0,T])
,
w^{\prime}(0)\geq 0
and
0\leq w\leq\eta
. Let us reason by contradiction and suppose that
w^{\prime}(0)=0
. We shall then prove that

\frac{1}{K^{-1}\circ F}\in L^{1}(0^{+}),

which contradicts (

¬

KO0
), completing the proof.

First, by Lemma
3.2
there exists
t_{0}\in[0,T)
such that
w(t)\equiv 0
on
[0,t_{0}]
while
w>0
on
(t_{0},T]
. If
t_{0}=0
then
w^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
by our assumption, while if
t_{0}>0
then
w(t_{0})=w^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
since
w
is
C^{1}([0,T])
. From (
3.20
) and since
\varphi^{\prime}>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
we infer the existence of
w^{\prime\prime}
in
(t_{0},T)
. Thus,
w
satisfies

\wp\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime})w^{\prime\prime}+\wp^{\prime}\varphi(w^{\prime})=\wp af(w)l(w^{\prime})\qquad\mbox{in }(t_{0},T).

(3.29)

We first suppose that
\wp^{\prime}\geq 0
on
[0,T_{0}]
. By (
3.12
) and (
3.4
),
w
is a solution of the inequality

w^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime})w^{\prime\prime}\leq af(w)w^{\prime}l(w^{\prime})\qquad\mbox{in }(t_{0},T).

Integrating on
[t_{0},t)
, with
t\in(t_{0},T]
we have

\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\frac{w^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime})w^{\prime\prime}}{l(w^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{t}af(w)w^{\prime}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq a_{1}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}f(w)w^{\prime}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Changing variables and using
w^{\prime}(t_{0})=w(t_{0})=0
we deduce

K(w^{\prime}(t))=\int_{0}^{w^{\prime}(t)}\frac{s\varphi^{\prime}(s)}{l(s)}\mathrm{d}s\leq a_{1}\int_{0}^{w(t)}f(s)\mathrm{d}s=a_{1}F(w(t)).

(3.30)

Assumption
f>0
on
(0,\eta_{0})
implies that
F>0
on
(0,\eta_{0})
. Having chosen
T_{1}\in(t_{0},T]
in such a way that
a_{1}F(w(T_{1}))<K_{\infty}
, we apply
K^{-1}
, rearrange and integrate to obtain

\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\frac{w^{\prime}(s)\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}\big(a_{1}F(w(s))\big)}\leq(t-t_{0})\qquad\forall\,t\in(t_{0},T_{1}].

(3.31)

Changing variables,

\int_{0}^{w(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{K^{-1}(a_{1}F(\tau))}\leq(t-t_{0})\qquad\forall\,t\in(t_{0},T_{1}].

(3.32)

By Lemma
3.6
property (
KO0
) holds, as claimed.

We are left to consider the case
\wp^{\prime}\leq 0
. Then, by (
3.29
) we deduce that
w^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
on
(t_{0},T)
, hence
w^{\prime}
is increasing there. Integrating (
3.29
) on
(t_{0},t)
and using the
C
-monotonicity of
f,l
together with
w^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\varphi\big(w^{\prime}(t)\big)&=&\displaystyle\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\wp(s)a(s)f(w(s))l(w^{\prime}(s))\mathrm{d}s\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle C^{2}a_{1}f(w(t))l(w^{\prime}(t))\left[\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\int_{0}^{t}\wp(s)\mathrm{d}s\right].\end{array}

(3.33)

Now, consider the energy
E(t)=K\big(w^{\prime}(t)\big)-a_{1}F\big(w(t)\big)
. Differentiating and using (
3.33
) and the definition of
a_{1}
, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}E^{\prime}(t)&=&\displaystyle\frac{w^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime})w^{\prime\prime}}{l(w^{\prime})}-a_{1}f(w)w^{\prime}=\frac{w^{\prime}}{l(w^{\prime})}\left[-\frac{\wp^{\prime}}{\wp}\varphi(w^{\prime})+af(w)l(w^{\prime})-a_{1}f(w)l(w^{\prime})\right]\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle-\frac{\wp^{\prime}}{\wp}\frac{w^{\prime}\varphi(w^{\prime})}{l(w^{\prime})}\leq\left|\frac{\wp^{\prime}(t)}{\wp(t)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\wp(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|C^{2}a_{1}f(w)w^{\prime}\\[11.38092pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{\wp_{1}T_{0}\|\wp^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{0}])}}{\wp_{0}^{2}}C^{2}a_{1}f(w)w^{\prime}=\bar{c}f(w)w^{\prime}.\end{array}

Integrating and using
w(t_{0})=w^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
,

K\big(w^{\prime}(t)\big)\leq a_{1}F\big(w(t)\big)+\bar{c}F\big(w(t)\big)=(a_{1}+\bar{c})F\big(w(t)\big).

Having obtained again an inequality like (
3.30
), to achieve the desired contradiction it is sufficient to repeat verbatim the last steps of the proof for
\wp^{\prime}\geq 0
.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

When
\wp^{\prime}\geq 0
, to reach the desired conclusion in
(ii)
we do not use the assumption that
l
is
C
-increasing.

Proposition
3.7
has a converse, at least if the threshold
\eta
in (
3.5
) is sufficiently small, namely (
KO0
) implies that
w^{\prime}(0)=0
. To reach the goal, the idea is to compare
w
with an explicit supersolution of (
3.5
), whose construction generalizes the End Point Lemma in
[
123
, Lem. 4.4.1]
.

Proposition 3.9
.

Assume (
3.2
), (
3.3
) and (
3.4
). Suppose that

f(0)l(0)=0
;

f
is
C
-increasing on
(0,\eta_{0})
, for
\eta_{0}>0
as in (
3.2
);

l
is
C
-increasing on
(0,\xi_{0})
, for some
\xi_{0}>0
.

If
f>0
on
(0,\eta_{0})
and

\frac{1}{K^{-1}\circ F}\in L^{1}(0^{+}),

(KO
0
)

then there exists
\eta_{1}
sufficiently small that, for each
\eta\in(0,\eta_{1})
, the solution
w
of problem (
3.5
) constructed in Theorem
3.1
satisfies

w^{\prime}(0)=0.

(3.34)

Proof.

For
\sigma\in(0,1)
to be determined, using (
KO0
) and Lemma
3.6
we implicitly define
z(t)
by setting

t=\int_{0}^{z(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(\sigma F(s))}\qquad\text{for }\,t\in[0,T).

Note that
z
is positive on
(0,T)
. Differentiating,

z^{\prime}=K^{-1}(\sigma F(z)\big),

(3.35)

whence
z^{\prime}>0
on
(0,T)
and
z^{\prime}(0)=0
. Evaluating
K
on both sides of (
3.35
) and differentiating once more we get

\frac{z^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}(z^{\prime})z^{\prime\prime}}{l(z^{\prime})}=\sigma f(z)z^{\prime}\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T).

Since
\varphi^{\prime},l,z^{\prime}
are positive, we deduce
z^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
, and simplifying by
z^{\prime}
we infer

\big(\varphi(z^{\prime})\big)^{\prime}=\sigma f(z)l(z^{\prime}).

Integrating, using
z^{\prime}(0)=0
together with the
C
-increasing property of
f
and
l
(note that
z^{\prime}
is increasing) we obtain

\varphi\big(z^{\prime}(t)\big)=\displaystyle\sigma\int_{0}^{t}f\big(z(s)\big)l\big(z^{\prime}(s)\big)\mathrm{d}s\leq\sigma C^{2}Tf\big(z(t)\big)l\big(z^{\prime}(t)\big).

Summarizing, so far we have obtained,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle\big[\wp\varphi(z^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}=\wp\big(\varphi(z^{\prime})\big)^{\prime}+\wp^{\prime}\varphi(z^{\prime})&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{\sigma}{a_{0}}\left[1+C^{2}T\frac{\|\wp^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{0}])}}{\wp_{0}}\right]\wp af(z)l(z^{\prime})\\[11.38092pt]
&=&\displaystyle\frac{1}{2C}\wp af(z)l(z^{\prime}),\end{array}

where we have defined
\sigma
in order to satisfy the last equality. Next, we fix
\eta_{1}\leq z(T)
small enough in such a way that each
\eta\in(0,\eta_{1})
meets the requirements in Theorem
3.1
, to guarantee the existence of
w
. We claim that, for
\eta\in(0,\eta_{1})
, the solution
w
in Theorem
3.1
satisfies
w\leq z
on
(0,T)
. This, together with the already established
z^{\prime}(0)=0
, forces
w^{\prime}(0)=0
and concludes our proof. By contradiction, suppose that
c=\max_{[0,T]}(w-z)>0
and let
\Gamma=\{w-z=c\}
. By construction,
\Gamma\Subset(0,T)
, and since
w,z
are
C^{1}
we deduce
w^{\prime}=z^{\prime}
on
\Gamma
. By continuity and since
w^{\prime}>0
on
(0,T)
, we can choose
\delta\in(0,c)
close enough to
c
in such a way that
l(z^{\prime})\leq 2l(w^{\prime})
on the set
I_{\delta}=\{w-z>\delta\}
. On
I_{\delta}
, using the
C
-increasing property we therefore have

\big[\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}\geq a\wp f(w)l(w^{\prime})\geq\frac{1}{2C}a\wp f(z)l(z^{\prime})\geq\big[\wp\varphi(z^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}=\big[\wp\varphi((z+c)^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}

and
w=z+c
on
\partial I_{\delta}
. By standard comparison (one can apply, for instance Proposition
4.1
below to an appropriate radial model),
w\leq z+c
on
U_{\delta}
, contradiction.
∎

3.2
The mixed Dirichlet-Neumann problem

We next move to investigate the problem

\begin{cases}\big[\wp\,\varphi(w^{\prime})\big]^{\prime}=\wp af(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T),\\[5.69046pt]
w^{\prime}(0)=0,\qquad w(T)=\eta,\\[5.69046pt]
0\leq w\leq\eta,\qquad w^{\prime}\geq 0\ \text{ on }\,(0,T),\end{cases}

(3.36)

for given
\eta>0
,
T\in(0,T_{0})
. We here extend and generalize in several directions the core of Corollary 1.4 of
[
57
]
,
without requiring any monotonicity on
l
, as well as the results of Section 4 of
[
21
]
. We assume (
3.2
) and (
3.4
), and we define
a_{0},a_{1},\wp_{0},\wp_{1},f_{\eta},l_{\xi}
and
\Theta(T)
as in (
3.6
).

Theorem 3.10
.

Assume (
3.2
) and (
3.4
), and that

\begin{array}[]{l}f>0\quad\text{on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+},\qquad f(0)=0;\\[5.69046pt]
l>0\quad\text{on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}.\\[5.69046pt]
\end{array}

(3.37)

Then, for each
\eta,\xi>0
and
T\in(0,T_{0})
satisfying

\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}<\varphi(\xi),

(3.38)

the problem

\begin{cases}[\wp\,\varphi(w^{\prime})]^{\prime}=\wp af(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T)\\[5.69046pt]
w^{\prime}(0)=0,\qquad w(T)=\eta,\\[5.69046pt]
0\leq w\leq\eta,\qquad 0\leq w^{\prime}<\xi\ \text{ on }\,(0,T),\end{cases}

(3.39)

admits solution
w\in C^{1}([0,T])
, and there exists
t_{0}\in[0,T)
such that

w(t)\equiv w(t_{0})\geq 0\ \ \text{ on }\,[0,t_{0}],\qquad w^{\prime}>0\ \ \text{ on }\,(t_{0},T].

(3.40)

Moreover, if

\varphi\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),\qquad\varphi^{\prime}>0\quad\text{ on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+},

then
w\in C^{2}\big((t_{0},T]\big)
and satisfies

\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime})}{l(w^{\prime})}\,w^{\prime\prime}=a\,f(w)-\frac{\wp^{\prime}}{\wp}\cdot\frac{\varphi(w^{\prime})}{l(w^{\prime})}\qquad\text{on }\,(t_{0},T).

(3.41)

All of the above conclusions still hold if condition
\wp>0
on
[0,T]
, in (
3.4
), is replaced by

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\wp>0\quad\text{on }\,(0,T],\qquad\wp(0)=0\\[5.69046pt]
\wp^{\prime}\geq 0\quad\text{on }\,[0,\delta),\ \,\text{ for some }\,\delta>0.\end{array}

(3.42)

Remark 3.11
.

Differently from the Dirichlet problem, if we allow
l
to vanish at
t=0
in the Neumann case we cannot guarantee that the solution of (
3.39
) be non-constant. This motivates the necessity to require
l>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
.

Proof.

The strategy goes along the same lines as that for the Dirichlet problem. First, we redefine
f
outside of
[0,\eta]
and
l
outside of
[0,\xi]
in such a way that

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle f\in C(\mathbb{R}),\qquad\displaystyle 0\leq f(s)\leq f_{\eta}\quad\text{ for }\,s\geq\eta,\qquad f(s)=0\quad\text{ for }\,s<0,\\[5.69046pt]
l\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}),\qquad 0<l(s)\leq l_{\xi}\quad\text{ for }\,s\geq\xi\end{array}

(3.43)

This will not affect the conclusion of the proposition, since any ultimate solution
w
of (
3.39
), with
w\geq 0
,
w^{\prime}\geq 0
in
[0,T]
, satisfies
0\leq w\leq\eta
and
|w^{\prime}|<\xi
.

Denote with
X
the Banach space
X=C^{1}\big([0,T]\big)
, endowed with the usual norm

\|w\|=\|w\|_{\infty}+\|w^{\prime}\|_{\infty}
. Define the homotopy
\mathcal{H}:X\times[0,1]\to X
by

{\mathcal{H}}[w,\sigma](t)=\sigma\eta-\int^{T}_{t}\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\wp(s)}\int^{s}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\mathrm{d}s.

(3.44)

We claim that
\mathcal{H}
is well defined and valued in
X
. Indeed, in our assumptions

0\leq\frac{\sigma}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\Theta(T)f_{\eta}l_{\xi}<\varphi(\xi),

(3.45)

hence the term in round brackets in (
3.44
) lies in the domain of
\varphi^{-1}
and

\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]^{\prime}(t)=\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\in[0,\xi).

(3.46)

Furthermore,
\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]^{\prime}
is continuous on
[0,T]
, hence
\mathcal{H}
is valued in
X
. By construction,
\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma](T)=\sigma\eta
and
\mathcal{H}[w,0]=0
. From

0\leq\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\leq f_{\eta}l_{\xi}\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,

we deduce that
\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]^{\prime}(0)=0
. Fix
\sigma\in(0,1]
, and let
w
be a solution of
w=\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]
. We claim that
w(0)\geq 0
: otherwise, since
w(T)=\sigma\eta>0
there would exist a
first point
t_{1}\in(0,T)
such that
w<0
on
[0,t_{1})
and

w(t_{1})=0
, and therefore
f(w(t))=0
on
[0,t_{1}]
. Thus,
w^{\prime}\equiv 0
on
[0,t_{1}]
by (
3.46
), that is,
w
would be constant on
[0,t_{1})
, contradicting
w(0)<0=w(t_{1})
. From
w\geq 0
we also deduce
w^{\prime}\geq 0
on
[0,T]
by (
3.46
). Also, (
3.46
) implies that
\varphi(w^{\prime})
is of class
C^{1}\big([0,T]\big)
, and then from (
3.44
) that
w
is a classical weak solution of the problem

\begin{cases}[\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})]^{\prime}=\sigma\wp af(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T),\\[5.69046pt]
w^{\prime}(0)=0,\quad w(T)=\sigma\eta\\[5.69046pt]
0\leq w\leq\sigma\eta,\qquad 0\leq w^{\prime}<\xi\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T).\end{cases}

(3.47)

In particular, for
\sigma=1
,
w
is the desired solution of (
3.39
). To prove (
3.40
), let
\sigma=1
. From
w(T)=\eta
and
w^{\prime}\geq 0
we infer the existence of a minimal
t_{0}\in(0,T)
such that
w>0
on
(t_{0},T]
. Since
f>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
l>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
and
a>0
on
[0,T]
, a solution of
w=\mathcal{H}[w,1]
satisfies

w^{\prime}(t)=\mathcal{H}[w,1]^{\prime}(t)=\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)>0\qquad\forall\,t\in(t_{0},T].

If
t_{0}\neq 0
, by the monotonicity and non-negativity of
w
we get
w=0
on
[0,t_{0}]
. To show (
3.41
), using
\varphi(w^{\prime})\in C^{1}([0,T])
,
\varphi^{\prime}>0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
w^{\prime}>0
on
(t_{0},T]
in (
3.46
) we deduce
w^{\prime}\in C^{1}((t_{0},T])
. Identity (
3.41
) immediately follows by expanding the derivative in (
3.39
).

We assert that a solution of
w=\mathcal{H}[w,1]
exists, using again the Browder version of the Leray-Schauder theorem (see
[
58
, Thm 11.6]
).

To begin with, as already observed
\mathcal{H}[w,0]\equiv 0
for all
w\in X
. We next show that
\mathcal{H}
is
continuous on
X\times[0,1]
. Indeed, consider a sequence
\{(w_{k},\sigma_{k}\}\subset X\times[0,1]
, with

w_{k}\to w
in
X
and
\sigma_{k}\to\sigma
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. By continuity,
\sigma_{k}f(w_{k})l(|w_{k}^{\prime}|)\to\sigma f(w)l(|w^{\prime}|)
, and so
\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]\to\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]
by (
3.44
) and Lebesgue convergence theorem, as required. Next we show that
\mathcal{H}
is compact. To this aim, let
\{(w_{k},\sigma_{k})\}
be a bounded sequence in
X\times[0,1]
. From (
3.46
),

\|\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}\|_{\infty}<\xi,

(3.48)

and thus, since
\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}](T)=\sigma\eta\in[0,\eta]
,
\{\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]\}
is equi-bounded in
X
. We shall prove that
\{\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}\}
is equicontinuous. Set

\begin{array}[]{l}I_{k}(t)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w_{k}(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau,\\[11.38092pt]
x_{k}=\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{k}I_{k}(t)}{\wp(t)},\qquad y_{k}=\frac{\sigma_{k}I_{k}(s)}{\wp(s)},\end{array}

(3.49)

and note that

\big|\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}(t)-\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}(s)\big|=\big|\varphi^{-1}(x_{k})-\varphi^{-1}(y_{k})\big|,

(3.50)

and that, by (
3.45
),

0\leq x_{k},y_{k}<\varphi(\xi),\qquad|I_{k}(s)-I_{k}(t)|\leq c|s-t|\quad\text{with }\,c=\wp_{1}a_{1}f_{\eta}l_{\xi}.

(3.51)

Since
\wp>0
on
[0,T]
and there it is
C^{1}
, we deduce

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\displaystyle|x_{k}-y_{k}|&=&\displaystyle\sigma_{k}\left|\frac{\wp(t)I_{k}(s)-\wp(s)I_{k}(t)}{\wp(t)\wp(s)}\right|\leq\left|\frac{\wp(t)I_{k}(s)-\wp(s)I_{k}(t)}{\wp(t)\wp(s)}\right|\\[14.22636pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\frac{|\wp(t)-\wp(s)|}{\wp(s)\wp(t)}I_{k}(s)+\frac{|I_{k}(s)-I_{k}(t)|}{\wp(t)}\\[14.22636pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\left[\frac{cT}{\wp_{0}^{2}}\left(\max_{[0,T]}\wp^{\prime}\right)+\frac{c}{\wp_{0}}\right]|s-t|=\kappa|s-t|.\end{array}

(3.52)

Given
\varepsilon>0
, let
\varrho=\varrho(\varphi^{-1},\varepsilon)>0
be given by the uniform continuity of
\varphi^{-1}
on
[0,\varphi(\xi)]
. If
|s-t|<\varrho/\kappa
, then
|x_{k}-y_{k}|<\varrho
and thus, by (
3.50
),

\big|\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}(t)-\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}(s)\big|=\big|\varphi^{-1}(x_{k})-\varphi^{-1}(y_{k})\big|<\varepsilon,

(3.53)

proving the (uniform) equicontinuity of
\{\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}\}
. The compactness of
\mathcal{H}
then follows from Ascoli-Arzelá theorem.

To apply the Leray-Schauder theorem it remains to check the existence of a constant
L>0
such that
\|w\|\leq L
for each solution of
\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]=w
. But this immediately follows from properties (
3.47
), and indeed
\|w\|\leq\eta+\xi
.

It remains to consider the case when
\wp>0
on
[0,T]
is replaced by (
3.42
). From (
3.46
) and the monotonicity of
\wp
on
[0,\delta)
, we deduce that for
t\in(0,\delta)

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]^{\prime}(t)&=&\displaystyle\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)a(\tau)f(w(\tau))l(|w^{\prime}(\tau)|)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\\[14.22636pt]
&\leq&\displaystyle\varphi^{-1}\left(\frac{f_{\eta}l_{\xi}a_{1}}{\wp(t)}\int^{t}_{0}\wp(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)\leq\varphi^{-1}\left(f_{\eta}l_{\xi}a_{1}t\right).\end{array}

Hence,
\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]^{\prime}
is continuous up to
t=0
(i.e.
\mathcal{H}
is valued in
X
) and
w^{\prime}(0)=0
for each solution of
w=\mathcal{H}[w,\sigma]
. The rest of the proof follows verbatim, except for the equicontinuity of
\{\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}\}
that we now consider. By the monotonicity of
\wp
,

|x_{k}|\leq\frac{|I_{k}(t)|}{\wp(t)}\leq a_{1}f_{\eta}l_{\xi}t,

independently of
k
. Thus, given
\varepsilon>0
and the corresponding
\varrho=\varrho(\varphi^{-1},\varepsilon)
of the uniform continuity of
\varphi^{-1}
on
[0,\varphi(\xi)]
, we can choose
\vartheta\in(0,T/2)
independent of
k
such that
|x_{k}|<\varrho/2
if
t<2\vartheta
and
|y_{k}|<\varrho/2
if
s<2\vartheta
. Set

\hat{\wp}_{0}=\inf_{[\vartheta,T]}\wp>0,\qquad\hat{\kappa}=\frac{cT}{\hat{\wp}_{0}^{2}}\left(\max_{[0,T]}\wp^{\prime}\right)+\frac{c}{\hat{\wp}_{0}},

with
c
as in (
3.51
). Define

\bar{\vartheta}=\min\left\{\frac{\varrho}{\hat{\kappa}},\vartheta\right\}.

Let
s,t\in[0,T]
with
|s-t|<\bar{\vartheta}
. If
s,t\geq\vartheta
, then the chain of inequalities (
3.52
) holds verbatim with
\hat{\kappa},\hat{\wp}_{0}
replacing
\kappa,\wp_{0}
, respectively, and we deduce

|x_{k}-y_{k}|\leq\hat{\kappa}|s-t|<\varrho.

On the other hand, if one between
s,t
, say
t
, is less than
\bar{\vartheta}
, then from
\bar{\vartheta}\leq\vartheta
we deduce
s<t+|s-t|<2\vartheta
. Hence,
|x_{k}|<\varrho/2
and
|y_{k}|<\varrho/2
, and thus

|x_{k}-y_{k}|\leq|x_{k}|+|y_{k}|<\varrho.

In both the cases,
|x_{k}-y_{k}|<\varrho
and therefore (
3.53
) holds, proving the (uniform) equicontinuity of
\{\mathcal{H}[w_{k},\sigma_{k}]^{\prime}\}
.
∎

The next result relates condition
w(0)=0
to (
KO0
). In order to do so, we shall further require (
3.3
) in order to define
K
,
F
as in (
1.19
), (
1.20
).

Proposition 3.12
.

In the assumptions of Theorem
3.10
, suppose further (
3.3
) and that

\begin{array}[]{l}\wp^{\prime}\geq 0\qquad\text{on }\,(0,T),\\[5.69046pt]
\text{$f$ is $C$-increasing on $(0,\eta_{0})$, for some constant $\eta_{0}>0$.}\end{array}

If
w(0)=0
, then (
KO0
) holds.

Proof.

Because of the monotonicity of
\wp^{\prime}
and
w
, from (
3.39
) we deduce

\big(\varphi(w^{\prime})\big)^{\prime}\leq af(w)l(w^{\prime})\leq a_{1}f(w)l(w^{\prime})\qquad\text{for }\,t\in(0,T).

We now follow the steps in Proposition
3.7
: differentiating on
(t_{0},T)
, we deduce

K^{\prime}(w^{\prime})w^{\prime\prime}\leq a_{1}f(w)w^{\prime},

and integrating on
(t_{0},t)
with the aid of
w(t_{0})=w^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
we infer
K(w^{\prime})\leq a_{1}F(w)
. Let
t_{1}\in(t_{0},t)
be such that
a_{1}F(w)<K_{\infty}
for
t\in(t_{0},t_{1})
. This is possible, by continuity, since
F(w(t_{0}))=0
. Applying
K^{-1}
, integrating and changing variables we get

\int_{0}^{w(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(a_{1}F(s))}\leq(t-t_{0})\qquad\text{on }\,(t_{0},t_{1}),

and (
KO0
) follows from Lemma
3.6
.
∎

We next investigate the maximal interval of definition of
w
. Assume the validity of (
3.2
) and (
3.37
). To tie
w
with (
KO∞
), we assume (
3.3
) and

\frac{t\varphi^{\prime}(t)}{l(t)}\not\in L^{1}(\infty),

(3.54)

in order for
K
to be a homeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}
onto itself. We further replace (
3.4
) and (
3.42
) with

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\wp\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}),&\qquad\wp>0,\ \ \wp^{\prime}\geq 0\quad\text{on }\mathbb{R}^{+},\\[2.84544pt]
a\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}),&\qquad a>0\quad\text{on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}.\end{array}\right.

(3.55)

Fix
T>0
. Applying Theorem
3.10
we infer the existence of
w
solving (
3.39
) for each
\eta>0
sufficiently small (inequality (
3.38
) is always satisfied for small
\eta
since, by (
3.37
),
f_{\eta}\rightarrow 0
as
\eta\rightarrow 0
). From
w^{\prime}(T)>0
, we conclude that
w
can be extended on a maximal interval
[0,R)
. Our next task is to prove that, if the Keller-Osserman condition (

KO
∞

) is violated, then
R=\infty
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Assume (
3.2
), (
3.3
), (
3.37
) and (
3.54
). Let
a,\wp
satisfy (
3.55
). For a fixed
T>0
, consider the solution
w
of (
3.39
) for small positive
\eta
and let
[0,R)
be the maximal interval where
w
is defined. If
t_{0}
is as in Theorem
3.10
, then

w=w(t_{0})\quad\text{on }\,[0,t_{0}],\qquad w>0,\quad w^{\prime}>0\quad\text{on }\,(t_{0},R).

(3.56)

Furthermore, suppose that
f
is
C
-increasing on
(\bar{\eta}_{0},\infty)
for some
\bar{\eta}_{0}\geq 0
. If

\frac{1}{K^{-1}\circ F}\not\in L^{1}(\infty),

(
\neg
KO
∞
)

then
R=\infty
.

Proof.

Taking into account the sign of
f,l,a
, by (
3.39
)
\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})
is
C^{1}
and strictly increasing where
w
is positive, and from (
3.40
) it readily follows that
\wp\varphi(w^{\prime})>0
on
(t_{0},R)
. Properties (
3.56
) are then immediate from (
3.40
),
w^{\prime}(T)>0
and our assumptions on
\varphi
. Next, suppose by contradiction that
R<\infty
. We first claim that necessarily

w^{*}=\lim_{t\to R^{-}}w(t)=\infty,

(3.57)

where the existence of the limit is guaranteed by the monotonicity of
w
. To prove (
3.57
), assume by contradiction that
w^{*}<\infty
. Because of (
3.41
) and since
\wp^{\prime}\geq 0
,

\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime})w^{\prime\prime}}{l(w^{\prime})}\leq af(w)\leq a_{1}f(w)\qquad\text{on }\,(t_{0},R),

where we set
a_{1}=\|a\|_{L^{\infty}([0,R])}
. Multiplying by
w^{\prime}
, integrating on
(t_{0},t)
and changing variables we deduce
K(w^{\prime})\leq a_{1}F(w)
(we recall that
w^{\prime}(t_{0})=0
by (
3.39
)). Thus,
w^{\prime}
is bounded in
(R/2,R)
, namely
\|w^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq K^{-1}(a_{1}F(w^{*}))=L
. For
t,s\in(R/2,R)
, define
I_{k},x_{k},y_{k}
as in (
3.49
), and note that

|x_{k}|+|y_{k}|\leq 2a_{1}f_{w^{*}}l_{L}R=\bar{C}

Given
\varepsilon>0
let
\varrho=\varrho(\varphi^{-1},\varepsilon)
be given by the uniform continuity of
\varphi^{-1}
on
[0,\bar{C}]
. Proceeding as in (
3.52
) we deduce the existence of
\kappa>0
such that

|x_{k}-y_{k}|\leq\kappa|s-t|\qquad\text{for each }\,s,t\in\left(\frac{R}{2},R\right).

If
|s-t|<\varrho/\kappa
, then
|x_{k}-y_{k}|<\varrho
and so

|w^{\prime}(t)-w^{\prime}(s)|=\big|\varphi^{-1}(x_{k})-\varphi^{-1}(y_{k})\big|<\varepsilon.

In conclusion,
w^{\prime}
is uniformly continuous on
(R/2,R)
, and can be therefore extended by continuity at
t=R
. By the existence theory for ODEs,
w
would be further extendible beyond
R
, contradiction. This proves that
w(R^{-})=\infty
. Now, fix
T_{1}\in(0,R)
large enough that
w(T_{1})>\bar{\eta}_{0}
. Applying
K^{-1}
to inequality
K(w^{\prime})\leq a_{1}F(w)
on
(T_{1},R)
, rearranging, integrating on
[T_{1},t)
and changing variables we get

\int_{w(T_{1})}^{w(t)}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{K^{-1}(a_{1}F(s))}\leq t-T_{1}.

(3.58)

Since (

¬

KO∞
) holds and, by assumption,
f
is
C
-increasing on
(\bar{\eta}_{0},\infty)
, applying Lemma
3.6
we deduce that the left-hand side of (
3.58
) is unbounded as
t\rightarrow R^{-}
while the right-hand side is not, contradiction.
∎

4
Comparison results and the finite maximum principle

4.1
Basic comparisons and a pasting lemma

In this subsection, we collect two comparison theorems and a “pasting lemma” for
\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}
solutions that will be repeatedly used in the sequel. Throughout the section we assume

\varphi\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0}),\qquad\varphi(0)=0,\qquad\varphi>0\ \text{ on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}.\\

(4.1)

The first comparison is Proposition 6.1 of
[
110
]
, see also Theorem 2.4.1 of
[
123
]
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume (
4.1
) and that
\varphi
is strictly increasing on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Let
\Omega\subset M
be open, and suppose that
u
,
v\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)\cap C(\overline{\Omega})
solve

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta_{\varphi}u\geq\Delta_{\varphi}v\qquad\text{weakly in }\Omega,\\[8.5359pt]
\displaystyle u\leq v\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega,\end{array}\right.

(4.2)

and
\displaystyle\limsup_{x\in\Omega,\,x\rightarrow\infty}\big(u(x)-v(x)\big)\leq 0
if
\Omega
has non-compact closure. Then
u\leq v
in
\Omega
.

Our second comparison result is a special case of
[
7
, Thm. 5.6]
, see also
[
123
, Thm. 3.6.5]
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\varphi,f,l
satisfy

\begin{array}[]{l}\varphi\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})\cap C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),\qquad\varphi(0)=0,\qquad\varphi^{\prime}>0\quad\text{on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}\\[5.69046pt]
f\in C(\mathbb{R}),\qquad\text{$f$ is non-decreasing on $\mathbb{R}$,}\\[5.69046pt]
l\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})\cap\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),\qquad l>0\ \text{ on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}.\end{array}

Let
\Omega\subset M
be an open subset of a Riemannian manifold
M
, and fix
0<b(x)\in C(\overline{\Omega})
. If
u,v\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)\cap C(\overline{\Omega})
solve

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta_{\varphi}u\geq b(x)f(u)l(|\nabla u|)\qquad\text{on }\,\Omega,\\[5.69046pt]
\Delta_{\varphi}v\leq b(x)f(v)l(|\nabla v|)\qquad\text{on }\,\Omega,\\[5.69046pt]
u\leq v\qquad\text{on }\,\partial\Omega\\[5.69046pt]
\mathrm{ess}\inf_{K}\Big\{|\nabla v|+|\nabla u|\Big\}>0\qquad\text{for each }\,K\Subset\Omega.\end{array}\right.

(4.3)

Then,
u\leq v
on
\Omega
.

Remark 4.3
.

Condition
\mathrm{ess}\inf_{K}\big\{|\nabla v|+|\nabla u|\big\}>0
for each
K\Subset\Omega
cannot be avoided, as the counterexample in Remark 1, p. 79 of
[
123
]
shows. However, the restriction can be removed if
\Delta_{\varphi}
is strictly elliptic, see Section 3.5 of
[
123
]
for definitions and relevant results.

Remark 4.4
.

The underlying metric is not required to be smooth, and indeed a metric whose local matrix
g_{ij}
is continuous is sufficient.

Remark 4.5
.

It is worth to comment on
[
7
, Thm. 5.6]
. There, the authors consider solutions of more general quasilinear inequalities of the form

\mathrm{div}\mathbf{A}(x,\nabla u)\geq\mathcal{B}(x,u,\nabla u)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{div}\mathbf{A}(x,\nabla v)\leq\mathcal{B}(x,v,\nabla v),

for suitable Caratheódory maps
\mathbf{A},\mathcal{B}
. In our setting,

\mathbf{A}(x,\xi)=\frac{\varphi(|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\xi,

thus the positivity of
\varphi(s)/s
and
\varphi^{\prime}(s)
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
imply that the tangent map
\mathbf{A}_{*}
of
\mathbf{A}
is uniformly positive definite on compacta of fibers of
T\Omega\backslash\{\mathbf{0}\}\rightarrow\Omega
, a condition needed to apply Lemma 5.7 in
[
7
]
. The regularity of
\mathcal{B}
, defined at the end of p.592 therein, is equivalent to condition
l\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
.

To conclude, we discuss the pasting lemma. It is well-known that the maximum of two subharmonic functions is still subharmonic. For subsolutions of more general operators the situation is more delicate and we refer to
[
85
]
for a very general result, and to
[
17
, Appendix]
for an alternative approach via obstacle problems, in the setting of homogeneous operators. For our purposes, it is sufficient to consider the case in which one of the solutions is constant. The technique goes back to T. Kato in
[
79
]
, and has been generalized to a large class of quasilinear operators in
[
40
]
. The next result is special case of
[
40
, Thm. 2.1]
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Assume (
4.1
), and let
f\in C(\mathbb{R})
,
l\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})
with
f(0)l(0)=0
. Suppose furthermore than
b\in C(M)
. If
u\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M)
is a nontrivial solution of

\Delta_{\varphi}u\geq b(x)f(u)l(|\nabla u|)\qquad\text{on }\,M,

(4.4)

then
u_{+}=\max\{u,0\}
is a
\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M)
, non-negative solution of

\Delta_{\varphi}u_{+}\geq b(x)f(u_{+})l(|\nabla u_{+}|)\qquad\text{on }\,M.

Remark 4.7
.

Note that
u=0
is a solution of (
4.4
) since
f(0)l(0)=0
.

4.2
The finite maximum principle

We now prove Theorem
1.10
in the Introduction. For the convenience of the reader, we rewrite the assumptions and restate the result. We require

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\varphi\in C(\mathbb{R}^{+}_{0})\cap C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{+}),\qquad\varphi(0)=0,\qquad\varphi^{\prime}>0\ \text{ on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+};\\[5.69046pt]
\displaystyle\frac{t\varphi^{\prime}(t)}{l(t)}\in L^{1}(0^{+})\\[11.38092pt]
f\in C(\mathbb{R}),\qquad f\geq 0\quad\text{on }\,(0,\eta_{0});\\[8.5359pt]
l\in C(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}),\qquad l>0\ \text{on }\,\mathbb{R}^{+}.\end{array}\right.

(4.5)

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
M
be a Riemannian manifold, and assume that
\varphi,f
and
l
satisfy (
4.5
) and moreover

-

f(0)l(0)=0
;

-

f
is
C
-increasing on
(0,\eta_{0})
, with
\eta_{0}
as in (
4.5
);

-

l
is
C
-increasing on
(0,\xi_{0})
, for some
\xi_{0}>0
;

Fix a domain
\Omega\subset M
and let
0<b\in C(\Omega)
. Then,
(\mathrm{FMP})
holds on
\Omega
if and only if either

f\equiv 0\qquad\text{on }\,(0,\eta_{0}),

(4.6)

for some
\eta_{0}>0
, or

f>0\quad\text{on }\,(0,\eta_{0}),\quad\text{and}\qquad\frac{1}{K^{-1}\circ F}\not\in L^{1}(0^{+}).

(4.7)

Proof.

We recall that the validity of
(\mathrm{FMP})
means that for any solution
u\in C^{1}(\Omega)
of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Delta_{\varphi}u\leq b(x)f(u)l(|\nabla u|)\qquad\text{on }\,\Omega\\[5.69046pt]
u\geq 0\qquad\text{on }\,\Omega,\end{array}\right.

(4.8)

if
u(x_{0})=0
for some
x_{0}\in\Omega
then
u\equiv 0
. The argument follows the lines of the proof in
[
123
]
, with the help of a trick from
[
95
]
. We prove separately the sufficiency and necessity of (
4.6
),(
4.7
). First, having fixed a point
o\in\Omega
to be specified later, we choose
R,\kappa>0
such that
\overline{B_{2R}(o)}
does not intersect
\mathrm{cut}(o)
and

\mathrm{Ric}(\nabla r,\nabla r)\geq-(m-1)\kappa^{2}\qquad\text{on }\,\overline{B_{2R}(o)}\backslash\{o\},

where
r(x)=\mathrm{dist}(x,o)
. By the Laplacian comparison theorem (see Thm.
9.3
in Appendix A), denoting with
v_{g_{\kappa}}(r)
the volume of a geodesic sphere of radius
r
in a model manifold of sectional curvature
-\kappa^{2}

\Delta r\leq\frac{v_{g_{\kappa}}^{\prime}(r)}{v_{g_{\kappa}}(r)}=(m-1)\kappa\coth(\kappa r)\qquad\text{on }\,\overline{B_{2R}(o)}\backslash\{o\}.\\

(4.9)

Sufficiency of conditions (
4.6
) and (
4.7
)
.

For
o\in\Omega
and having set
R,\kappa
as above, fix
a_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that
